# 52 — Translation ledger, every upstream item against its Zig counterpart

**What this document is.** One row per public or `pub(crate)` item in the
pinned upstream `accesskit` and `accesskit-c` trees, with the upstream
`path:line`, the upstream name, the kind, the Zig `path:line`, the Zig
name, and a port decision. `AGENTS.md` section 6 requires this table.

**Where the inputs came from.** The upstream trees are at
`/tmp/scratch/upstream/`, fetched from `AccessKit/accesskit`
and `AccessKit/accesskit-c` at `refs/heads/main`. The item list was generated
by `gen.py` into a 1238-row inventory, and agents mapped it crate by crate
against the Zig tree, then re-examined every open row a second time.

**The four decisions.**

|Decision|What it means|Rows|
|---|---|---|
|ported|the Zig item exists and does the same job|457|
|adapted|ported, but the shape differs; the reason follows in the row|648|
|not-applicable|no counterpart and never will be; the reason follows in the row|194|
|open|missing or incomplete implementation. This is the remaining work.|57|

**The 57 open rows are the remaining translation work.** The table has 1356 rows.
Each open row records missing or incomplete implementation, or a written design decision.
A written decision does not count as an implementation. Most open rows remain
in the platform adapters. Three `atspi-common/adapter.rs` rows remain open.
The two Android decisions remain open until their source changes land.
The open rows comprise 39 Unix, 13 Windows, three AT-SPI common, and two Android rows.

The affected declaration coordinates refer to the current checked source.
They record source mappings, not platform execution or gate results.

**Duplicated upstream locations are expected.** The C ABI generates 353 of its
496 exported functions from `macro_rules!` invocations in
`accesskit-c/src/common.rs`, so many rows share one `common.rs:NNN` line.

|`accesskit-c-main/src/android.rs:69`|`android_adapter`|struct|`src/capi/handles.zig:67`|`accesskit_android_adapter`|ported (upstream is a `_private: [u8; 0]` marker struct; the C header forward-declares it opaque and Zig declares the same name as `opaque {}`)|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/common.rs:1070`|`action_data`|enum|`src/schema/update.zig:282`|`ActionDataTag + ActionDataPayload + CActionData`|adapted (upstream is a single `#[repr(C)] enum` with data, which cbindgen lowers to a `tag` field plus an anonymous union; the port splits it into three types: `ActionDataTag = enum(c_uint)` at :282 for the tag, `ActionDataPayload = extern union` at :313 for the eight members, and `CActionData = extern struct { tag: c_uint, payload: ActionDataPayload }` at :340 for the whole. A Zig `union(enum)` is not used, because that would put the tag inside Zig's own payload machinery and not at offset 0 of a C-visible struct. `action_request.zig:435-445` asserts the tag enum and the payload union have the same 8 members in the same order and that the tag is `c_uint` wide)|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/common.rs:1110`|`action_request`|struct|`src/schema/update.zig:363`|`CActionRequest`|ported (both are a four-field `extern struct`: action, target tree, target node, then the optional action data. Field order and types are identical)|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/common.rs:1134`|`ActivationHandlerCallback`|type|`src/capi/boundary.zig:63`|`ActivationHandler`|adapted (the C callback returns a nullable owning opaque accesskit_tree_update handle. Each nonnull result transfers ownership to the library. Shared conversion consumes the handle through its captured allocator on success and error. Null means no tree. Native adapter values remain schema.TreeUpdate)|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/common.rs:1141`|`FfiActivationHandler`|pub(crate) struct|`src/schema/handlers.zig:41`|`ActivationHandler`|adapted (upstream is a private struct holding a callback and a `Send` userdata wrapper, and it implements the Rust `ActivationHandler` trait; the port's `ActivationHandler` is a plain `struct` holding the `callconv(.c)` function pointer and the userdata, and the trait impl becomes a direct call. Not a public API item: the C ABI only sees the callback typedef above)|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/common.rs:1147`|`new`|pub(crate) fn|`src/schema/handlers.zig:41`|`ActivationHandler.init`|adapted (upstream's `FfiActivationHandler::new` unwraps the `Option` callback, which panics on `None`; the port builds the handler directly from a non-optional pointer, so there is no unwrap to write. Mapped to the struct's own construction rather than a free function, because upstream's is an inherent method)|

|`accesskit-c-main/src/lib.rs:113`|`BoxCastPtr`|pub(crate) trait|`src/capi/marshal.zig:1`|`allocation store + per-handle release functions`|adapted (upstream bundles three pointer-to-Box operations behind a trait with a blanket impl per handle, including `to_nullable_mut_ptr`; Zig has no `Box` and no trait, so the port puts the owned payload behind `src/capi/allocation.zig` and gives each handle its own release function in the module that creates it. The three operations survive as `accesskit_*_free` and the `*_new` functions, all of which are rows in this ledger|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/lib.rs:136`|`ref_from_ptr`|pub(crate) fn|`src/capi/marshal.zig:1`|`per-module conversion functions`|adapted (upstream is one generic function shared by every handle, with the return lifetime chosen by the caller. Zig has neither generic lifetime elision nor a shared generic pointer cast, so the port splits it: each C ABI module takes its own typed pointer parameter, and the cast is written at that module's boundary. There is no single counterpart function, which is why this row points at the layer rather than a line|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/lib.rs:144`|`mut_from_ptr`|pub(crate) fn|`src/capi/marshal.zig:1`|`per-module conversion functions`|adapted (as `ref_from_ptr`: the mutable form is likewise written at each module's boundary rather than shared)|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/lib.rs:151`|`box_from_ptr`|pub(crate) fn|`src/capi/allocation.zig:386`|`allocation.release + per-handle release functions`|adapted (upstream recovers a Box from a raw pointer and drops it. The allocation layer releases its own blocks through the declared allocator. Typed C handles use their per-handle release functions and captured allocators. The port has no shared Box conversion)|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/lib.rs:206`|`debug_repr_from_ptr`|pub(crate) fn|`src/capi/tree_info.zig:399`|`treeInfoDebug`|adapted (upstream is one generic function that formats any `Debug` value and hands the `CString` over. Zig has no `Debug` bound, so the port writes one formatter per type and three call sites: `src/capi/tree_info.zig:399`, `src/capi/tree_update.zig` for the update, and the node and adapter debug exports. All three say so in their own doc comments, citing this function by line|

|`accesskit-c-main/src/lib.rs:83`|`CastPtr`|pub(crate) trait|`src/capi/handles.zig:39`|`opaque {} handle types`|not-applicable (the trait's whole job is to record which Rust type a snake_case C handle maps to, so that casts can be written without `as` and without losing const-ness. Zig has no implicit pointer casts to police, so there is no such record to keep; each C ABI module does its own conversion through `src/capi/marshal.zig` and the handle is declared `opaque {}` at `src/capi/handles.zig`, which the module comment there states is what stops a C consumer reading through the pointer)|
|`accesskit-c-main/src/lib.rs:93`|`CastConstPtr`|pub(crate) trait|`src/capi/handles.zig:39`|`opaque {} handle types`|not-applicable (as `CastPtr`: it exists to stop an `Arc` being cast to a mutable pointer. The Zig port holds no `Arc` and has no const-tracking cast to guard, and the eleven handle types are `opaque {}` so the pointer is not dereferenceable from Zig's side either)|

|`accesskit-main/accesskit_consumer/src/text.rs:601`|`map`|fn|`src/consumer/text/range.zig:105`|`map`|adapted (a free function whose first parameter is the value, not a method: every other operation in consumer/text/range.zig is a free function, per the module doc at range.zig:9-19. The answer is RangePropertyValue(U) and not RangePropertyValue(?U), because the port's RangePropertyValue(T) is upstream's RangePropertyValue<Option<T>>: the ledger row for text.rs:595 records that the Option sits in the tag where upstream puts it in the type parameter, so upstream's impl block on RangePropertyValue<Option<T>> returning RangePropertyValue<Option<U>> is the port's impl on RangePropertyValue(T) returning RangePropertyValue(U). Upstream's f: impl FnOnce(T) -> U becomes a *const fn (T) U, because a Zig closure would carry state that upstream's parameter does not. Three tests at range.zig:1336, :1342 and :1346 pin the three answers text.rs:605-607 gives: a present value maps, mixed passes through untouched, and a single that holds no value stays single and empty because upstream's Option::map skips the call on None. docs/10-accesskit-core.md:2103 declares the method form pub fn map(self: RangePropertyValue(?T), f: *const fn (T) U) RangePropertyValue(?U); that row is a survey and it is superseded here, the same way the ledger adapts the other eleven text.rs operations from methods to free functions)|

|`accesskit-main/adapters/android/src/event.rs:235`|`ScrollDimension`|struct|`adapters/android/queued_events.zig:-`|`-`|open (issue #4 design decision recorded, implementation still required. Preserve `ScrollDimension { current: JInt, delta: JInt, max: ?JInt }` by value. Replace `QueuedEvent`'s three-field collapse with a tagged union for the existing simple/content-change events and a `scrolled` payload. The payload carries an optional node ID and independent `x: ?ScrollDimension` and `y: ?ScrollDimension` values. This keeps absent axes and maxima distinct from zero, without borrowed `NodeRef` values or payload allocations. Source evidence: [pinned event.rs:235-239,261-290,320-324](https://github.com/AccessKit/accesskit/blob/466e24e252103f4d82bd6fbab98141b551e46e0c/adapters/android/src/event.rs#L235-L324). In `adapter.zig:691`, read both old/new nodes and apply upstream's include filter before scroll work. Compare each axis only when both offsets exist, and enqueue one scroll event when either axis changes. Preserve the subtree event. Compute the delta in `i64`, then check the `JInt` conversion. Read each changed axis's optional maximum through `node.zig:619,639`. Represent a root scroll as `node = null`, so raise uses `HOST_VIEW_ID` through `queued_events.zig:343-346`. For nonroot nodes only, allocate the virtual ID through `node_id_map.zig:211` before enqueue. This prevents a skip for an unfilled node without replacing the host ID with a virtual ID. [Pinned util.rs:145-158](https://github.com/AccessKit/accesskit/blob/466e24e252103f4d82bd6fbab98141b551e46e0c/adapters/android/src/util.rs#L145-L158) establishes the root special case. [Pinned adapter.rs:101-105,143-169](https://github.com/AccessKit/accesskit/blob/466e24e252103f4d82bd6fbab98141b551e46e0c/adapters/android/src/adapter.rs#L101-L169) establishes the producer behavior. Widen `ChangeContext.append_error` at `adapter.zig:677` to a named optional `SlotError` combining allocator, narrowing, ID-map, and `consumer.tree.QueryError` errors. The include filter at `adapter.zig:170-175` calls `consumer/filter.zig:268-270` and can return `TreeCycle`. Keep the first exact tag and stop later slot queue work after failure. After the tree commits, return that tag and dispose of the incomplete queue. Do not retry the consumed update or collapse `OutOfRange` into `OutOfMemory` or `TreeEventQueueFailed`. Update the Android type record in `AGENTS.md:318` with this change. This follows its tagged-error rule at :309-326 rather than freezing the present allocator-only set. In `queued_events.zig:301`, dispatch the union and preserve upstream's setter order: `setScrollable(Z)V`, then current/delta/optional maximum for X, then Y. The six numeric setters use `(I)V`. Copy the scalar payload intact through `injecting.zig:481-499`. Consume raise reports and exact failure tags in `injecting.zig:492` and `root.zig:219`, while keeping the C signatures and exactly-once queue release. Use the accessibility gate and exception-stop contract in the util.rs:245 row below. Update all constructors, callers, member tables, descriptor evidence, and census assertions together, including `queued_events.zig:435`. Do not classify newly documented descriptors as unpinned merely to preserve old counts. The reachable next requirement is consumer-visible tests, then this source cutover. Tests must cover each axis, optional maxima, unchanged/missing offsets, checked delta overflow, exact slot errors after commit, root-scroll `HOST_VIEW_ID` delivery, pending-queue payload preservation, and JNI setter order. A filter failure must preserve its exact tag after the committed update. The collapsed struct suffices for payload-free events, but cannot represent upstream's scroll payload. The tagged union is the chosen direction for payload fidelity. No code implements this decision yet)|

|`accesskit-main/adapters/android/src/util.rs:245`|`is_accessibility_enabled`|fn|`adapters/android/queued_events.zig:-`|`-`|open (issue #4 design decision recorded, implementation still required. The manager class is documented, not unavailable. [Android's AccessibilityManager API](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/view/accessibility/AccessibilityManager#isEnabled()) names `android.view.accessibility.AccessibilityManager` and `public boolean isEnabled()`. Its JNI class descriptor is `Landroid/view/accessibility/AccessibilityManager;`, its internal name is `android/view/accessibility/AccessibilityManager`, and the method descriptor is `()Z`. These follow the documented class and [JNI type-signature rules](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/specs/jni/types.html#type-signatures). [Context.ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/Context#ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE) documents `getSystemService(String)` as the manager receiver source. [Pinned util.rs:208-250](https://github.com/AccessKit/accesskit/blob/466e24e252103f4d82bd6fbab98141b551e46e0c/adapters/android/src/util.rs#L208-L250) supplies the exact calls: `View.getContext()Landroid/content/Context;`, `Context.getSystemService(Ljava/lang/String;)Ljava/lang/Object;` with `"accessibility"`, then manager `isEnabled()Z`. Choose one receiver lookup per nonempty raise through the injected `JniTable`. Use `java_call.callObjectChecked` and `callBooleanChecked` at `java_call.zig:136,188`. Release the context, service string, and manager local references on every path. Do not retain a manager, cache an enabled result, copy upstream's file-scope `OnceLock<GlobalRef>`, or add `getObjectClassFn`. The documented class works with the existing `FindClass` seam. Per-raise lookup costs JNI calls but needs no global owner or teardown and uses the current host context. An adapter-owned global reference would save lookups but add a lifetime obligation, so this decision rejects it. Gate `queued_events.zig:301` before parent/package lookup or event creation. An empty queue needs no gate calls. If disabled, send nothing, count all events as skipped, and let the caller dispose of the queue without retry. [Pinned event.rs:344-355](https://github.com/AccessKit/accesskit/blob/466e24e252103f4d82bd6fbab98141b551e46e0c/adapters/android/src/event.rs#L344-L355) requires UI-thread raise and explains the disabled-state exception risk. Implement the helper as `java_call.CallError!bool`, never as false-on-error. Null receivers report `JavaMemberMissing`, string allocation reports `OutOfMemory`, and pending exceptions report `JavaException`, following `java_call.zig:57-65,95-147`. Extend `RaiseReport` at `queued_events.zig:215` with `failure: ?java_call.CallError`, preserving the first exact tag beside the counts. Gate failure counts the whole queue as failed and sends nothing. Any pending exception during gate or event delivery stops further JNI work except permitted reference cleanup. Count unsent remaining events as failed. Do not clear the exception, retry, or attempt later events on that thread. Preserve already-raised counts and bound the dispatch loop by the entry queue length. Both `root.zig:219-226` and `injecting.zig:492-499` must consume the report and store its failure in the owning adapter's `last_error`. Both paths must release the consumed queue on success, disabled state, and failure. Add the manager and the two new gate members to `classes.zig`, `methods.zig`, and the raised-member census with these source links. Reconcile `queued_events.zig:435` and its census tests with the documented descriptors, not guessed unpinned totals. The reachable next requirement is tests for enabled/disabled delivery, empty queues, all acquisition failures, exception stop, exact tags, and local-reference cleanup, then implementation. No code implements this decision yet)|

|`accesskit-main/adapters/windows/src/node.rs:404`|`name`|fn|`src/adapters/windows/change_handler.zig:526`|`nodeHasName`|ported (upstream `NodeWrapper::name` at `node.rs:404-413` writes the value for a node whose label comes from its value and `write_label` for every other node, and answers `Some` exactly when that write wrote something. The UTF-16 `WideString` result has no counterpart because the port's strings are UTF-8 from `schema` down, and the two change-handler call sites only need presence (`isLiveRegion` at `change_handler.zig:613`) and equality (`namesDiffer` at `change_handler.zig:563`), so `nodeName` at `change_handler.zig:541` returns a UTF-8 buffer and the encoding is not observable. The port was wrong here before this change: `isLiveRegion` read `strings.rawLabel(node) == null`, which is the node's own stored label and nothing else, so a node named by a `labelled_by` contributor and a `label`-role node carrying a value were both nameless. The test at `change_handler.zig:1444` pins all three cases and the handler call site)|
|`accesskit-main/adapters/windows/src/node.rs:52`|`NodeWrapper`|struct|`-`|`-`|open (no counterpart: upstream `NodeWrapper` at `node.rs:52-55` is the pair `node: &NodeRef` and `string_buffer: &mut Vec<u16>`, and every one of its roughly fifty methods reads or writes through one of the two. The ledger row for `accesskit-main/adapters/android/src/node.rs:27` records the same dissolution for the Android wrapper, where the whole wrapper became free functions; here only the two methods the change handler reaches were needed. The remaining methods build `VARIANT` values for the `UIA_*PropertyId` domain and belong to the node layer, which `root.zig:17-30` records as absent and which cannot be checked on this host because `com.zig:35-37` records that none of the thirteen vtables is written)|

The ledger row was open because the match sits outside the file that declares `NodeWrapper`; the two tests at `change_handler.zig:1244` and `:1348` pin every role in the match, and the second sweeps the whole `schema.Role` domain so a role added later is named without an edit)|
|`accesskit-main/adapters/windows/src/node.rs:669`|`is_selected`|fn|`src/adapters/windows/change_handler.zig:595`|`nodeIsSelected`|ported (transcribed arm for arm as `nodeIsSelected` at `change_handler.zig:595-611`: the radio arm reads `properties.toggled(node)` and answers true only for `.true_`, and every other role answers `properties.isSelected(node) orelse false`. Upstream `node.rs:672-674` is `self.node.toggled() == Some(Toggled::True)` and `:677-678` is `self.node.is_selected().unwrap_or(false)`. The port was wrong here before this change: all four call sites read `properties.isSelected(node) orelse false`, which is `accesskit_consumer/src/node.rs:912-914` and never reads `toggled`, so a radio button that states `aria-checked=true` and no `aria-selected` was not selected. The call sites are `change_handler.zig:354` in `nodeAdded`, `:371` in the filtered-out arm of `nodeUpdated`, and `:387-388` in the rest of `nodeUpdated`, and the test at `change_handler.zig:1395` pins both the predicate and the `nodeAdded` call site)|
